# Triage table — Tape Pool `ceed0a2`

Owner decision checkpoint. These are house triage recommendations, not the final audit reports or deployment approval. No production change has been made. Read the numbered owner questions below before adopting disputed acceptances.

Merged entries: **207**. Findings: **12** (0 Critical, 1 High, 1 Medium, 10 Low, 0 Info). Below threshold: **0**. Accepted risks: **27**. Rejected: **164**. Leads: **4**, unscored.

The ten units, every named critic candidate and every conflict/break row were reconciled. All **100 Slither results** have a numbered mapping; “none new” is not their triage decision. Duplicate raw alerts remain in the provenance records, not in the count. One key represents one cause at one location. An allegation rejected at a gate is not promoted merely because it was repeated; repeated demoted compatibility/arithmetic areas are promoted under §5. Passing local characterization is distinguished from a completed real-chain loss trace.

[Detailed triage basis and strongest input write-ups](TRIAGE-EVIDENCE.md), [all entry data](../runs/triage-entries.json), [conflict/break mapping](../runs/conflict-mapping.json), [Slither mapping](../runs/slither-mapping.json), [test index](../runs/test-index.json), and [work integrity](../runs/integrity.json) preserve the review trail. Leads state their missing step, deciding evidence and hard-bound effect in the detailed basis. Accepted-risk rows name §16 and state their scope; operational assertions remain coverage gaps.

## Questions for the owner

1. **Restored income — Q1.** Does §16’s “up to 122 BEM” bound the risk you accepted, or did you intend to accept pro-rata redistribution of any restored amount? The reproduced entrant gain is **249.44962306 BEM**, while the per-wave restored income can approach the **500 BEM** principal bound and repeated waves have no lifetime cap. Please choose a fix or explicitly accept the enlarged economic scope. Also supply the original inputs and meaning of “moves” for the 0.0046/4.3 examples; neither is reproduced from its original fixture.
2. **Stale healthy overwrite — Q2.** Was it deliberate that a stale or insane healthy report can erase B and revive an older positive VC, even though no fresh H or newer VC arrives? The actual receiver/agent test restores **1 BEM**. Does the §16 phrase “for as long as it stays the stored report” accept this outcome, or should such an overwrite remain unknown/blocking? The Medium recommendation is promoted at75; a full authenticated producer-to-lender-loss trace is still incomplete.
3. **Emission cutoff — Q3.** Is the **2,592,000-second (30-day)** early cutoff deliberate? The script pins **1910726132**, while start+period is **1913318132**. Choose the normative first-halving pin or explicitly approve an earlier borrowing shutdown. It is retained as Low until that decision.
4. **Reserve allocation — Q4.** Should reserve fees accumulate a proportional share across fragmented payments, or is flooring each payment intentional? The500-raw fixture gives reserve100 in one payment and0 across125 payments. Also confirm that the stored per-loan cap row accepts payment-order differences across loans: the supplied fixture gives reserve1 versus0.6 BEM with the same6 BEM fees. Both leave total backing unchanged; one diverts dust cumulatively and the other follows the stored-cap/global-reserve rule.
5. **Replacement policies and quote inputs — Q5.** Does the supported domain include uint256-max debt limits, gaps in allowed debts, partial evaluator disagreement, and runtime malformed policy answers after a canonical activation probe? Is the lens required to authenticate the pool’s factory for any supplied real clone, or must the client do it? Existing code/ABI contains no downward-closure or finite-limit requirement. The reproduced Low findings stay pending; this question does not assume the missing app validates inputs.
6. **Conformance wording — Q6.** Please settle: (a) zero late rate until December7 as an operational commitment versus a calendar guard; (b) unreadable listed-voucher withdrawal as successful forgetting versus retain/revert; (c) inbound BEM pulls as exceptions to blanket CEI; (d) zero-amount entry during doubt and “every cap” at zero assets; (e) live-only flag extension, NoLoan versus NoDebt, closed-agent forwarding, B/U/S freshness exceptions, and the replacement-policy exception to the v1 ceiling. These rejected or accepted entries are preserved rather than silently described as conformant.
7. **Deployment gate — Q7.** Will deployment attest the actual reporter build or use a discriminating multiplier-above-one sample? The targeted full-gate retry passes for the incompatible earlier runtime and demonstrates384 H instead of24 H. Lender loss from that wrong build is unproved; the Low gate finding is reproduced.

## Coverage gaps and ledger reconciliation

Every ledger table row containing “undecided” is listed below, including incidental references in evidence and matrix cells: **1493 rows**. **83 settled**, **24 partly settled**, and **1386 unresolved**: **1410 rows retain a gap**. Of these, **1281** are system state/order rows that do not assert an independent expected joint result. These counts are ledger rows, not vulnerabilities. The complete original row text is preserved in [coverage-reconciliation.json](../runs/coverage-reconciliation.json). A “settled” row can settle as a finding or rejected conflict, rather than as holds.

**Current execution limits.** The first imported run reported957 passed /57 failed /0 skipped (148 suites). Of57 initial failures,53 were RPC403, one was RPC429, one was a repeated RPC-page failure and two lacked required fork-block environment variables. Targeted retries set the documented public block inputs, and produced7/7 and2/0 passed/failed results. The timeout, pre-pinned library and wrong-build gate now reproduce. The original unit run passed383/383. Setup failures can conceal multiple test bodies; a failed setup is never counted as a passed suite. Exact per-suite failures and later passes are in the test index. Historical BSC archives, intermittent X Layer reads, the complete old-stack census and several full source/destination lifecycles remain unverified in this run. No production regression assertion failed in these runs; this is not a claim that all review suites are green.

Across these four runs there are **1349 passing executions** and **1344 distinct passing (path, contract, test) identities**. Inherited duplicates across review contracts are distinct identities, not independent attack confirmations. GateDryRun is separate: exit1 is the expected negative control, with zero endpoint calls instead of the required one. [Run commands](../runs/RUN-COMMANDS.md) and raw logs preserve initial failures.

Actual-fork successes include the X Layer evaluator on all4096 vectors, real BEM transfer/allowance and repayment samples, active/stopped BSC harvest production, voucher compose/record writes, and feed lock checks. These narrow the external gaps; they do not establish every historical pin, real DVN authentication, revoked transition, pending payout or Safe authorization route.

**Scope limits.** App, keeper, proof tool, omitted runbooks/historical documents and actual signing/key custody are absent. No missing document was fetched. The full external Safe/upgrade/registration authorization trees, actual DVN honesty/liveness, future implementation ABIs, live extreme credit inventory, forecasts and original numerical risk fixtures are not established. Some sequence matrices observe differences without a rule-specific joint oracle. Deliberate source mutants from the input reviewers were inspected as evidence; they were not rerun by editing this work copy. The stateful invariant campaign and every original fork suite were not rerun in full. The supplied commit label has no local work Git history for independent authentication.

**Pass-count qualification (§2).** “Seen in” lower bounds are recorded per entry in the detailed basis. A supplied characterization plus this triage provides at least two tool executions for each finding; no person has confirmed one yet. Exact k/N by review execution cannot be reconstructed reliably from consolidated FINDINGS files and continuation/recheck documents. SCAN-FACTS gives turn totals but no per-entry execution ledger. Unit counts, copied assertions, inherited tests and repeated descriptions are not substituted for pass counts. This remains a report-provenance gap to resolve or disclose before writing the house reports. The single supplied Slither run also falls short of §2’s two-runs-per-tool workflow; it was fully triaged without inventing a second run.

**Method limits.** The supplied prep facts describe a coordinator familiar with earlier reviews, neutral unit briefs without focus hints, a21-minute earlier zero-finding pass,29 wrong-setting turns subsequently redone,94 total turns,4 content-filter stops,9 capacity failures and two machine restarts. Some input reviewers read an installed skill; this triage did not. Calibration found weakness where literal specification conformance hides an economic fault; Q1/Q2/Q4 explicitly revisit that weakness. Input ACCEPT verdicts and a clean earlier pass are not safety evidence. No human has yet confirmed this triage; the owner checkpoint and later separate report reader remain pending. Full prep facts will be printed in the reports only after authorization to continue.

**Work integrity.** 372 original files compared unchanged, including32 source files. Added tests are confined to the authorized audit directory; imports were adapted using [import-adaptations.json](../runs/import-adaptations.json). Build/cache/log artifacts are under out. No live transaction, installation, download, commit or push was performed.
